This made him realise that all the science up till that time—and all those customary ideas about life, mortality, etc., which had been based upon that science—needed radical revision. He could not understand why so many who talked about Einstein and relativity were prepared to accept it simply as a tool for practical application in science without being intrigued, as he had been, by its wider and more profound, philosophical implications. Nor, on the other hand, could he understand those who regarded relativity as an esoteric to be expropriated by a privileged few. Such knowledge, he felt, should be part of the corpus of common understanding, which it obviously could not be whilst it remained shrouded in mathematics and specialised jargon. This drove him, for conscientiously democratic reasons, to develop for himself an independent commonsense interpretation of the facts of relativity and to follow their implications wherever logically they might lead. With the conviction that what nature tells one person it may also tell another, he set about re-discovering for himself, by ordinary commonsense means, what Einstein had reported about observational time and space. This he managed to do, in a way which was verified by mathematicians and physicists but which put a very different philosophical slant on some of Einstein’s findings. In particular, it put in question Einstein’s interpretations of gravitation, light-velocity and inter-atomic action-at-a-distance.

Viewed as an ‘assault on Einstein’ made by a complete outsider, the thesis could not be pursued in any academically approved way. This book has been an account of the fortunes of that heretical thesis, describing on the one hand, the help given in its development by some esteemed individuals such as Karl Popper—and, of course, Einstein himself—and, on the other hand, its rejection by the Educational Establishment which hindsight revealed to be perfectly understandable. This rejection was not simply because the thesis observed none of the departmental distinctions between Arts and Science, Physics and Philosophy, Mechanics and Psychology, etc. It was mainly because of the unclassifiable nature of the problem it had addressed, of the implied relativistic time-staggering of momentary consciousnesses—with which, so far as he knew, no one had ever systematically dealt. ‘I’m not qualified to answer that question’ was a sentiment much echoed, not only by physicists but also by philosophers.

But no more ‘qualified’, for that matter, was a telephone engineer. Indeed, there seemed to be no ideal ‘qualifications’ anywhere in the system for pursuing such a logical enquiry. Yet those questions were definitely there, aching to be answered. So he decided to satisfy his own curiosity, regardless of all academic lets and hindrances, by following the natural logical trail into those realms that are normally regarded as too ‘philosophical’ by hard-headed scientists and too ‘scientific’ for philosophers and theologians.

In short, the whole thrust of the work is that when perfectly logical enquiries into nature do not fit the academic provisions, then so much the worse for those provisions—and so much more the need for radical change.

APPENDIX 1

To test the relativistic formula, in his typical 'engineering-drawing' style of reasoning, our telephone lineman took a pair of dividers and a ruler. With these he produced the following set of scale diagrams to represent the motion, in an otherwise empty space, of three observers with telescopes trained on one another throughout the motion. These observers he called 'O', 'P' and 'Q'. Taking O's point of view first: as P, coming from afar, whizzed by him at a steady speed of, in this sample case, 0.6 of the speed of light, at their closest proximity the two observers synchronise their watches to read time zero. After one second of his time, travelling at that speed away from O, P passes the third observer, Q coming towards him, approaching O at that same speed of 0.6 c relative to O. As they pass close to each other, Q synchronises his watch with that of P so that, on reaching O his time-reading will be that of P as if P had gone all the way from O and back with a magical turnaround —i.e., without involving any forces of deceleration and acceleration.

The diagrams representing these three different views of the motion in turn are as published in the Proceedings of the Alternative Natural Philosophy Association (ANPA), 1994, as an Appendix to a presented paper [138]. That Appendix, with the diagrams (Figure 2 in that paper) is reproduced below. The diagrams were drawn in the same way as his original ones. that is, with the scale of distance-travelled to time taken being in the ratio of units c = 186,000 miles to the second. In the diagrams, therefore, the times of the (half-second) clock ticks, at where they occurred and where they were seen by the other observers, were compared by means of the arrowed dotted lines. The diagrams constructed in this way depict the whole motion-event as witnessed by each of the three participants in turn. These scale drawings made it doubly plain that there was no symmetry of the sort he had assumed in his original letter to Einstein, since at the end of the motion, as in all the intermediate positions, all three observers concur on the differences in their respective time-readings. So there was no doubt about it, plain commonsense, engineering-type representations of the motion corroborated Einstein's mathematics.

[134] Pope, N. V., 'The Relativistic Clock Non-Paradox', in Appendix to 'Normal Realism: A Challenge to Physicists', Proc. of ANPA 15, Sept. 1993, Cambridge, pp. 109-130. pp. 128-130.

APPENDIX 2

To Hell with Education

A Denunciation of Scholasticism in Science

(With apologies to C. S. Lewis' 'The Screwtape Letters'.)

Once, there dashed into hell a young devil, to tell Some news he could scarcely contain. 'There's been a disaster!' he called to the Master, 'I fear all our work is in vain!'

Drawled Satan: 'Keep cool, you impetuous fool. I find such excitement uncouth!' 'But Dad,' cried the lad, 'this really is bad. Mankind's found a Great Bag of truth!'

'I stayed hid,' he said, 'as the pieces were spread All jigsaw-like, there on the Table. And some, in a bit, tried the pieces to fit As cleverly as they were able.'

'So what?' drawled Old Nick, 'Do you seriously think Such a trifle deserves my attention? This knowledge they've found, I'll bet you a pound, Will never become comprehension!'

The lad was astonished. 'But Dad,' he admonished, 'This news ought to make us suspicious! We surely decline to let Truth Divine Mar Ignorance truly perditious!'

'Relax!' said his Pa, 'It will not get them far. In me you can place your reliance.' 'How so?' asked the boy, and the sage said, 'My ploy Is to let all that truth become Science.'

'I am not satisfied,' young Nicky replied, 'This answer of yours is surprising!' 'Then listen, dear youth. For each who loves truth There are thousands who love organising.'

'It happens like this: at first it is bliss To pick pieces out of the pile And find where they fit, near the Chair where you sit At the Table. Then, after a while

The bits you abort, by others are sought To fit where those others are seated. And soon, as the spaces between all those places Are filled, so the aim is defeated.

It's like this, my son. Though it's barely begun, The project must end in frustration. Because, by this time, the whole "pantomime" Is wrapped-up in Administration.

Their trouble, you see, is that though truth is free, They don't see my hellish perversity. By evil intent I've let them invent, What aspirants call "university".

'This makes the poor fools establish new rules So marvellously diabolical That scholars for fees can pursue "Ph.D.s" For purposes non-philosophical.

Departments are named and probably famed For keeping their "jigsaws" intact. With sole occupation to forge reputation For knowledge both full and exact.

In this way they save all the truth-bits that they've Put together by efforts intense. But the total of which (and here is the hitch) Can make no overall sense.

'Now you and I know that the best way to go To restore the rate of advance Is withdraw the stricture on keeping each 'picture' Where placed by its first circumstance.

To move chunks around is the plan that is sound Till they fit beyond any question. But guardians of knowledge ensconced in each college Can contemplate no such suggestion.

"There's no way," they'll say, "such a plan to obey In keeping with scholarly purity. In Disciplines tight we maintain our right To our places, our pay and security.

It must never appear that we interfere In areas controlled by our peers. We say, and with pride, that we're 'not qualified' In matters outside our own spheres."

'So these institutions, by circumlocutions, The aim of the game they are changing. There are gaps where no bit can possibly fit While the puzzle resists rearranging.

In those gaps they neglect, we neatly inject, For their aims now wholly sophistical Our Hellish infusion of sheer illusion And fancies entirely mystical.

They easily make the fatal mistake Of thinking the form ineluctable And thus we ensure, by methods secure, That Truth is no longer constructable.

You see, then, my son, how our evil is done No matter how mankind has toiled Or what it has cost them, true Wisdom is lost them. And that is how Goodness is foiled.' ■

APPENDIX 3 (Relating to Chapter VII)

Deriving Balmer from Einstein

In the relativistic time-formula, viz.:

$$t _ { \mathrm { R } } = t \left( 1 - v ^ { 2 } / c ^ { 2 } \right) ^ { - 1 / 2 }$$

the relative time $t _ { \mathrm { R } }$ is some number times the proper time $t$. Now we may suppose that in physics there is no continuum of numbers as there is in pure mathematics. If real physical quantities formed a continuum, then since the ultimate units of that continuum would be infinitesimals, the sum of those infinitesimals would always be zero, so that there could be no non-zero physical measures. In that case, multiples of a physical measure such $t$, whether those multiples be whole or fractional, may be represented by a ratio $n / N$ of whole numbers $n$ and $N$ (pace Diophantus $^{[139]}$). Starting with the fraction $1 / N$ of $t$, therefore, (1) becomes

$$t _ { \mathrm { R } } = n ( t / N ) = ( t / N ) \left( 1 - v ^ { 2 } / c ^ { 2 } \right) ^ { - 1 / 2 }$$

Let $t$ in this equation be $1 / c R$, where $c R$ is the frequency-coefficient of the spectrum of hydrogen ($R$ is the well-known Rydberg number for the hydrogen spectrum). Substituting accordingly in (2) produces:

$$t _ { \mathrm { R } } = n / N c R = ( 1 / N c R ) \left( 1 - v ^ { 2 } / c ^ { 2 } \right) ^ { - 1 / 2 }$$

from which may be derived the following frequency equation:

$$( v ^ { 2 } / c ^ { 2 } ) N ^ { 2 } c R = N ^ { 2 } c R ( 1 - 1 / n ^ { 2 } )$$

For intermediate values of $n$ and with the frequency-term on the left of the equation signified by $v$ ($n u$) we now have:

$$v = N ^ { 2 } c R [ ( 1 - 1 / n _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } ) - ( 1 - 1 / n _ { 1 } ^ { 2 } ) ]$$

where $n _ { 1 }$ is the fixed term of the series and $n _ { 2 }$ is the running term, equal to or greater than $n _ { 1 }$. By simplification we then obtain

$$v = N ^ { 2 } c R [ ( 1 / n _ { 1 } ^ { 2 } - 1 / n _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } ) ]$$

which is the standard generalised formula for the frequencies of hydrogen-like atoms (ions) of atomic number $N$, as determined empirically by Balmer and Rydberg.

$^{[139]}$ Diophantus, a Greek mathematician, circa A.D. 270. Equations such as these, expressing relations between whole numbers are called, by mathematicians, Diophantine.

APPENDIX 4

The Cosmological Redshift

It seemed to our odysseyist that from a purist view of science, the integrity of contemporary science had been compromised by a 'holy alliance' which some physicists had contrived between the Biblical story of The Creation and the so-called 'Expanding Universe'. Omitting to specify the observer of the supposed universal expansion made that 'observer', by default, the 'God' of the deists. By this undeclared metaphysical assumption these physicists, it seemed, had duped themselves and the Church authorities (to the extent, in some cases, of being supported out of Church funds) into believing that they had somehow succeeded in reconciling their mechanistic cosmism with Christianity. This was as absurd as though geologists had been accredited by the American Congress with finding parallels between the theory of continental plate tectonics and the United States Constitution! How could the Church have imagined that such a synthesis of deism and theism could be anything but a fallacy? How could they have been so gullible as to be persuaded that their God's moral designs for life and humanity described in the articles of the Christian faith could be cashed out in terms of intrinsically blind mechanical processes?

Moreover, as he saw it, that loss of objectivity on the part of Science was compounded by an astounding alacrity to talk about 'the expanding universe' as though it were a plain observational fact. What we observe, in that astronomical context, is never a 'recession of the galaxies', as is so often reported, but a systematic shift, first observed by Edwin Hubble, in 1929, towards the red in the spectral lines in the images of galaxies in proportion to their calculated distance. On the basis of that logical howler — that because motion of recession causes redshifts, all redshifts are caused by motions of recession — all sorts of 'corroborative evidence' had, of course, been gathered in support of that 'universal expansion'. He felt, however, that not even in politics could such a fallacy have gone so long and so blatantly ignored.

In any truly objective assessment of the situation it was plain that only if it could be shown beyond reasonable doubt that there is no possible explanation of that redshift other than as a Doppler effect due to motion, could the 'Big Bang' interpretation be taken as anything but a dogma, and that Doppler interpretation had by no means been proved to the satisfaction of all concerned. Certainly, if it is assumed, as in Einstein's Special Relativity, that the light from those galaxies travels in vacuo at the 'constant speed $c$', then there can be no other explanation, because if all parts of the light-wave travel at the same speed in the way Einstein envisaged, then there can be no stretching of the wavelength between the source and the observer other than by the nodes of the wave being emitted from progressively further distances as the source travels away, as in the typical Doppler effect.

However, if one did not accept the Einsteinian hypothesis that the constant $c$ is an objective velocity of that space-travelling sort — as one well might, as he had discovered, without creating contradiction — then that condition of the equality of the speed of all wave-elements relative to one another in vacuo disappears. In his own theory, a light-interaction between an emitter and absorber has two length-components, or geodesics. One is the instantaneous angular momentum connection $s$, described in XI.4 and the other is the time- or sequence-component $t_R = s/c$. The first of these components, $s$, being instantaneous, cannot be anything but straight. However, the possibility could not be excluded that for a constant distance-separation $s$ there might well take place, under certain conditions, a bending — and therefore a stretching — of the geodesic $t_R$ between two relatively stationary points. A normal distribution of cumulative non-linearities over intergalactic distances, therefore — the possibility of which, again, could not be excluded — might account for the observed redshifts, providing a steady-state alternative to the 'expanding universe' explanation. Any arbitrary exclusion of this distinct possibility was, accordingly, a dogma by definition. ■
